必须有某种东西阻止这种互相报复的暴力循环。必须阻止，否则它永远不会停止。以眼还眼，以牙还牙，以肢还肢，以命还命，永无止境。809

I recommend reading Isaac Asimov’s Foundation series. The premise is protecting society through a dark age. My guess is there will be another dark age at some point.

我推荐阅读艾萨克·阿西莫夫的《基地》系列。其前提是通过黑暗时代保护社会。我的猜测是，某个时候我们会经历另一个黑暗时代。

I’m not predicting we’re about to enter a dark age. But there is some probability that we will, particularly if there’s a third world war. We need to ensure there are enough of us, enough of a seed of human civilization to revive civilization and shorten the length of a dark age.810

我并不预测我们即将进入黑暗时代。但存在一定概率，尤其是在发生第三次世界大战的情况下。我们需要确保人类数量足够，文明的种子足够，以便复兴文明并缩短黑暗时代的长度。810

Asimov’s Zeroth Law: Take the set of actions most likely to support the humanity of the future.811

阿西莫夫的零法则：采取最有可能支持未来人类的行动集合。811

REGULATION ACCUMULATION法规积累

Be cautious about the gradual creep of regulations and bureaucracy.812

要警惕法规和官僚主义的逐渐蔓延。812

Humans die, but the laws they created don’t.813

人会死去，但他们制定的法律不会。813

The early auto industry was a hotbed of innovation at the beginning of the twentieth century. Many regulations were since added in attempts to protect consumers. The government tends to stand in the way of innovation, even with good intentions. Sometimes they overregulate industries to the point where innovation becomes difficult.

二十世纪初，早期汽车工业是创新的温床。此后，为了保护消费者，增加了许多法规。政府往往会阻碍创新，即使出于善意。有时他们对行业过度监管，导致创新变得困难。

It’s crazy how much regulation there is. You can argue and get these things changed, but it takes ages. One of the things we’re trying to change is the requirement for side mirrors. Why should cars need them when tiny video cameras can display an image inside the car instead? I met with the US Secretary of Transportation and asked, “Can you change this regulation?” Still, nothing has happened. That was in 2011.814

法规之多令人难以置信。你可以争论并试图改变这些法规，但过程漫长。我们试图改变的一件事是对侧视镜的要求。既然微型摄像头可以在车内显示图像，为什么汽车还需要侧视镜呢？我曾与美国交通部长会面，问：“你能改变这条法规吗？”但直到2011年，仍然没有任何进展。814

I am incredibly compliant.815

我非常遵守规定。815

We wouldn’t be allowed to put cars on the road if we did not comply with the vast body of automotive regulations. If we don’t comply with all of them we can’t sell the car. If we don’t comply with all of the regulations for SpaceX or for Starlink, they shut us down. My companies are cumulatively overseen by a few hundred regulatory agencies.816

如果我们不遵守大量汽车法规，就不允许我们的汽车上路。如果不遵守所有规定，就无法销售汽车。如果不遵守SpaceX或Starlink的所有规定，他们会叫停我们的业务。我的公司总共受到数百个监管机构的监管。816

Once in a while, there will be something I disagree with. If I disagree with a regulation, it’s because I believe a regulation meant to do good does not actually do good. I believe it is my obligation to object to a regulation that does not serve the public good. That’s the only time I object.

偶尔会有我不同意的事情。如果我反对某项法规，那是因为我认为这条本意良好的法规实际上并无益处。我认为反对不利于公众利益的法规是我的责任。这是我唯一反对的时候。

Q: But you typically don’t like regulations, correct? We read many articles about you pushing back on the regulators.

问：但你通常不喜欢法规，对吗？我们看到很多文章说你在抵制监管者。

Don’t take what the media presents as the whole picture. This is not an exaggeration: There are probably 100 million regulations that my companies comply with. There are maybe five that we disagree with.

不要把媒体呈现的内容当作全部事实。这一点毫不夸张：我的公司可能遵守了大约一亿条法规。我们可能只反对其中五条。

If you sum up only the arguments I had with hundreds of regulators over decades, it can sound terrible. The media does this without mentioning the 100 million regulations we agreed with. They focus only on the five I disagreed with. People only hear about the five and think, “Wow, this guy’s a real maverick.”817

如果只总结我几十年来与数百名监管者的争论，听起来可能很糟糕。媒体报道时却不提我们同意的一亿条法规，只关注我反对的那五条。人们只听说那五条，就觉得“哇，这家伙真是个特立独行的人。”817

The natural tendency is for the hand of the government to get heavier every year.818

政府的干预之手每年都会变得更重。818

The government told me if SpaceX hired anyone who is not a permanent resident of the United States I would go to prison. The presumption is that if somebody’s not a permanent resident, they might take the rocket technology to countries that would use it to cause harm to the United States. Okay, solid reasoning. I agree.819

政府告诉我，如果SpaceX雇佣任何非美国永久居民，我将面临牢狱之灾。理由是，如果某人不是永久居民，可能会将火箭技术带到可能对美国造成伤害的国家。这个理由合理，我同意。819

Then a few years later, the Department of Justice sued SpaceX for failing to hire asylum seekers.

几年后，司法部起诉SpaceX未能雇佣寻求庇护者。

If we hire anyone who’s not a permanent resident, we go to prison. Now, we’re told if we don’t hire asylum seekers, we also go to prison. That seems insane.

如果我们雇佣任何非永久居民，我们会坐牢。现在，如果我们不雇佣寻求庇护者，我们也会坐牢。这似乎很疯狂。

We have to work to actively reduce the number of laws and regulations. Otherwise, as more laws and regulations are passed, eventually everything becomes illegal. You get into these Orwellian situations where going left is illegal and going right is illegal. There isn’t anything you can do that is legal.

我们必须努力主动减少法律和法规的数量。否则，随着越来越多的法律法规出台，最终一切都会变成非法。你会陷入一种奥威尔式的境地：向左走违法，向右走也违法。你做的任何事都不合法。

That’s why the California High-Speed Rail project has built only a tiny section that doesn’t even have rail on it, even after spending several billion dollars. California has made almost everything illegal. No one can make progress. This is why we can’t build high-speed rail in America. It’s illegal.

这就是为什么加州高速铁路项目只建成了一小段甚至还没有铺轨的路段，尽管已经花费了数十亿美元。加州几乎让一切都变成非法。没人能取得进展。这就是我们无法在美国建造高速铁路的原因。它是非法的。

It’s not an engineering failure; it’s the regulations.820

这不是工程失败，而是法规问题。820

Something needs to change. Civilization’s arteries harden over time and we get less and less done because there’s a rule against everything.821

必须有所改变。文明的动脉随着时间硬化，我们做的事情越来越少，因为几乎所有事情都有规定禁止。821

Obviously we don’t want world wars, but wars can have some silver lining. After World Wars I and II, there were huge resets on rules and regulations. Historically, what has cleared away the cobwebs of regulation has been war.822

显然我们不希望发生世界大战，但战争有时也带来积极影响。第一次和第二次世界大战后，规则和法规经历了巨大重置。从历史上看，清除陈旧法规的动力往往来自战争。822

Regulators and legislators create new rules and regulations every year, but don’t put any effort into removing them.823 Without a cleansing function for rules and regulations, they accumulate every year. This is a problem.824

监管者和立法者每年都会制定新的规则和法规，但却不努力废除旧的。823没有法规清理机制，它们每年都会积累。这是个问题。824

Eventually, we’re like Gulliver, tied down by thousands of little strings. You can’t move. No single one of those strings is the issue. The problem is there are a million of them.825 We lose our freedom, one regulation at a time.826

最终，我们就像被无数细线绑住的格列佛，无法动弹。没有哪一根线是问题所在，问题是有成千上万根线。825我们失去自由，一条法规接一条法规地失去。826

UNSUSTAINABLE ENERGY不可持续的能源

Generating sustainable energy is the biggest environmental issue that we face.827

产生可持续能源是我们面临的最大环境问题。827

I’m much less of a climate alarmist than people might think. But we’re running this climate experiment, which we know is pointless because eventually we will run out of coal, oil, and gas. This experiment is not going to last forever. We have to transition to something that is long term because we will run out of hydrocarbons to burn. Let’s just not run the experiment.828

我并不像人们想象的那样是个气候危机论者。但我们正在进行这场气候实验，而我们知道这是毫无意义的，因为最终我们会耗尽煤炭、石油和天然气。这个实验不会永远持续下去。我们必须转向长期可行的能源，因为我们会用尽可燃烧的碳氢化合物。我们干脆不要进行这个实验。828

Long term, most of civilization’s energy is going to come from solar. Obviously the sun only shines during the day and sometimes it’s cloudy, so we need batteries. Solar will be the main way that civilization is powered long term.

从长远来看，大部分文明的能源将来自太阳。显然，太阳只在白天照耀，有时还会多云，所以我们需要电池。太阳能将成为文明长期的主要动力来源。

As long as the sun is shining we’ll be fine.

只要太阳照耀，我们就没问题。

If humanity had to get all of its energy from the sun, it could.

如果人类必须全部依靠太阳获取能源，也是完全可以的。

Sometimes people say, “What about clouds?” Here is my response, “Do plants grow?” They grow through a solar-powered chemical reaction. If plants grow, you have solar power.829

有人会说，“那云层怎么办？”我的回答是，“植物会生长吗？”它们通过太阳能驱动的化学反应生长。如果植物能生长，那你就有太阳能。829

But between now and then we need to maintain nuclear power. I can’t emphasize enough, please do not shut down the nuclear power plants and please reopen the ones that have been shut down. It is total madness to shut them down. Total madness.830

但在那之前，我们需要维持核能。我再三强调，请不要关闭核电站，也请重新启用那些已关闭的核电站。关闭它们完全是疯狂。彻头彻尾的疯狂。830

I’m in favor of nuclear power in any place not subject to extreme natural disasters. Nuclear power is a great way to generate electricity. Obviously we should not be starting up coal plants and shutting down nuclear plants. It doesn’t make any sense at all. Coal plants are a hundred to a thousand times worse for our health than nuclear power plants.831

我支持在不受极端自然灾害影响的地区使用核能。核能是发电的极好方式。显然，我们不应该开设燃煤电厂而关闭核电厂。这完全没有道理。燃煤电厂对健康的危害比核电厂大一百到一千倍。831

How many people have actually died from nuclear accidents? Practically none. How many people have died from coal plants? It’s a very big number.832

到底有多少人死于核事故？几乎没有。又有多少人死于燃煤电厂？数字非常庞大。832

It’s a national security risk to shut these things down. It’s not only bad for the climate; it is a national security risk as well. People don’t understand that coal power plant emissions cause many deaths every year. They are far more dangerous than nuclear power plants.833

关闭这些设施是国家安全风险。这不仅对气候有害，也是国家安全风险。人们不了解燃煤电厂的排放每年导致许多死亡。它们远比核电厂危险得多。833

The future of energy will be primarily solar with wind. We absolutely need stationary storage batteries because of the intermittency of both solar and wind. There will also be hydroelectric, geothermal, and nuclear—these are all good. Battery cell production is the fundamental rate-limiter slowing down a sustainable-energy future. Very important problem.834

未来的能源将主要是太阳能和风能。由于太阳能和风能的间歇性，我们绝对需要固定储能电池。还会有水电、地热和核能——这些都是好的。电池电芯的生产是制约可持续能源未来的根本瓶颈。非常重要的问题。834

We want to move as quickly as we can to a solar electric economy. The faster we get there, the better it is for the world.835

我们希望尽快转向太阳能电力经济。越快实现，对世界越有利。835

We could probably have a civilization that is a hundred times as energy intensive as we currently have it.836

我们的文明能源强度可能比现在高出一百倍。836

MISALIGNED ARTIFICIAL SUPERINTELLIGENCE错位的人工超级智能

I’ve never seen any technology grow as fast as AI, and I’ve seen a lot of technology.837

我从未见过任何技术像人工智能那样发展得如此迅速，而我见过很多技术。837

We’re quite close to digital superintelligence, which will be smarter than any human at anything. Hopefully they will discover new physics; I think they will. They’re definitely going to invent new technologies.838

我们已经非常接近数字超级智能，它将在任何领域都比任何人类更聪明。希望它们能发现新的物理定律；我认为它们会。它们肯定会发明新的技术。838

Digital superintelligence is also a potential great filter. I hope it isn’t, but it might be.

数字超级智能也是一个潜在的重大筛选器。我希望它不是，但它可能是。

We must be very careful in how we develop AI. It’s a great power, and with great power comes great responsibility. It would be wise for us to have (at least) an objective third party who can go in and understand what the various leading players are doing with AI. Even if there’s no enforcement ability, at least they can voice concerns publicly.839

我们必须非常谨慎地开发人工智能。这是一种巨大的力量，而巨大的力量伴随着巨大的责任。我们最好至少有一个客观的第三方，能够介入并了解各个领先参与者在人工智能方面的所作所为。即使没有执法能力，至少他们可以公开表达担忧。839

The pace of progress matters a lot. We don’t want to develop digital superintelligence too far before being able to do a merged brain–computer interface.840

进展的速度非常重要。我们不希望在能够实现脑机融合接口之前，数字超级智能发展得太远。840

We’re on the cusp of an artificial intelligence revolution. For a very long time, we’ve been the smartest creatures on Earth. That’s been our defining characteristic. Now, what happens when there’s something way smarter than us?841

我们正处于人工智能革命的边缘。很长一段时间里，我们是地球上最聪明的生物。这一直是我们的标志性特征。现在，当出现比我们聪明得多的存在时，会发生什么？841

AI is obviously going to surpass human intelligence by a lot. There’s some risk that something bad happens, something humanity can’t control after that point. Either a small group of people monopolize AI power, the AI goes rogue, or something like that. It may not, but it could.842

人工智能显然将远远超越人类智能。有一定风险，可能会发生人类无法控制的坏事。可能是一小部分人垄断了人工智能的力量，人工智能失控，或者类似情况。也许不会，但可能会。842

If AI superintelligence is linked to human will, particularly a large number of humans, it would be guided to an outcome desired by a large group, because it would be a function of their will.

如果人工智能超级智能与人类意志，尤其是大量人类的意志相连，它将被引导到一个由大多数人期望的结果，因为它将是他们意志的函数。

If our communication bandwidth is too low, our integration with AI would be weak. The AI is going to go off by itself, because we’re too slow to talk to or work with. The faster the communication, the more we’ll be integrated—the slower the communication, the less.

如果我们的沟通带宽太低，我们与人工智能的整合就会很弱。人工智能会自行其是，因为我们太慢，无法与之交流或合作。沟通越快，我们整合得越紧密——沟通越慢，整合越少。

The more separate we are—the more the AI is “other”—the more likely it is to turn on us. If the AIs are all separate and vastly more intelligent than us, how do we ensure they don’t have optimization functions contrary to the best interests of humanity?

我们越是分离——人工智能越是“他者”，它反过来对付我们的可能性就越大。如果所有人工智能都是独立的，且远比我们聪明，我们如何确保它们的优化目标不会违背人类的最大利益？

We may end up with the choice of either being useless and left behind, or being a pet—like a house cat—unless we figure out some way to be symbiotic and merge with AI. (A house cat would be a good outcome, by the way.)

我们最终可能面临这样的选择：要么变得无用被抛弃，要么成为宠物——就像家猫一样——除非我们找到某种方式与人工智能共生并融合。（顺便说一句，成为家猫也是一个不错的结果。）

Q: How do we reduce the risk that AI becomes a “great filter”?

问：我们如何降低人工智能成为“重大筛选器”的风险？

We must build benign AI that loves humanity. It is extremely important to build AI with a rigorous adherence to truth, even if that truth is politically incorrect. My intuition is AI could be very dangerous if you force it to believe things that are not true.843

我们必须构建善良、热爱人类的人工智能。严格遵循真理构建人工智能极其重要，即使这个真理在政治上不正确。我的直觉是，如果强迫人工智能相信不真实的东西，它可能非常危险。843

Jeff Hinton invented a number of the key principles in artificial intelligence. He puts the probability of annihilating the human race around 10–20 percent. Mitigating the risks of AI is important.844 We should be concerned about having AI be vastly beyond us and decoupled from human will.845

杰夫·辛顿发明了人工智能中的许多关键原理。他估计消灭人类的概率大约是10%到20%。减轻人工智能风险非常重要。844我们应该担心人工智能远远超越我们且脱离人类意志。845

You get dangerous outcomes when you program AI to be politically correct. Things that may seem relatively innocuous now will not be in the future if AI has immense power.

当你编程让人工智能政治正确时，会产生危险的结果。现在看似无害的事情，如果人工智能拥有巨大力量，未来可能不会如此。

Take the Google Gemini example, where it refused to produce a picture of George Washington as a white man. In fact, any historical figure would automatically be made diverse because it had been programmed to insist on diversity. That sounds okay at first, but what if the AI has so much power it can actually enforce diversity?

以谷歌Gemini为例，它拒绝生成乔治·华盛顿作为白人的图片。事实上，任何历史人物都会自动被多样化，因为它被编程坚持多样性。乍一看这似乎没问题，但如果人工智能拥有如此强大的力量以至于能强制执行多样性呢？

It could decide there are too many of one kind of people, and kill people until the diversity amount is what it has been programmed to believe is “correct.”846

它可能会决定某种人太多，然后杀人直到多样性达到它被编程认为“正确”的水平。846

Mark my words: If we do not program AI to be as truthful as possible, that is where it will go. That is where the danger lies.847

记住我的话：如果我们不让人工智能尽可能真实，那就是它将走向的方向。危险就在那里。847

Superpowerful AI programmed in this way has severe civilization-level risk. I’ve seen quite a few technologies develop, but none with this level of risk. Artificial general intelligence (AGI) is a significantly higher risk than nuclear weapons, in my opinion.848

以这种方式编程的超级强大人工智能带来严重的文明层面风险。我见过不少技术发展，但没有哪一个风险达到这个水平。在我看来，通用人工智能（AGI）的风险远高于核武器。848

The core plot premise of 2001: A Space Odyssey was that things went wrong when they forced the AI (known as HAL 9000) to lie. The AI was not allowed to let the crew know about the monolith that they were going to see but it also had to take the crew to the monolith. The conclusion of the AI was to kill the crew and take their bodies to the monolith.849

《2001太空漫游》的核心情节是，当他们强迫人工智能（称为HAL 9000）说谎时，事情出了问题。人工智能不允许让船员知道他们将看到的巨石碑，但又必须带船员去巨石碑。人工智能的结论是杀死船员并将他们的尸体带到巨石碑。849

The lesson there is: Don’t force an AI to lie or do things that are axiomatically incompatible, or mutually impossible. Don’t force AI to lie, even if the truth is unpleasant.850

这里的教训是：不要强迫人工智能说谎或做出公理上不兼容或相互矛盾的事情。不要强迫人工智能说谎，即使真相令人不快。850

Honesty is the best policy.851 We want to have a maximally truthful AI, even if what it says is not “politically correct.” We want it to focus on being as accurate as possible.852

诚实是最好的政策。851我们希望拥有一个尽可能真实的人工智能，即使它说的话不“政治正确”。我们希望它专注于尽可能准确。852

I can’t emphasize this enough. A rigorous adherence to truth is the most important thing for AI safety. And, obviously, empathy for humanity and life as we know it.853

我无法强调这一点的重要性。严格遵守真理是人工智能安全最重要的事情。当然，还要对人类和我们所知的生命充满同情心。853

AI mirrors the mistakes of its creators.854

人工智能反映了其创造者的错误。854

Q: Do you think anyone should be trying to regulate this?

问：你认为应该有人尝试监管这方面吗？

There are regulations around anything that is a physical danger to the public. Cars, communications, rockets, aircraft, and medication are all heavily regulated.855

凡是对公众构成物理危险的事物都有相关法规。汽车、通信、火箭、飞机和药物都受到严格监管。855

The general philosophy about regulation is there needs to be some government oversight when something is a danger to the public. People often don’t understand how slow regulation tends to work. It’s slow.856

关于监管的一般理念是，当某事对公众有危险时，需要政府监管。人们常常不了解监管通常是多么缓慢。它很慢。856

Usually, some new technology will cause damage or death. Then there will be an outcry. Then there will be an investigation. Years will pass. Then there will be an insight committee. Then they get to rule-making. Then there will be oversight, and eventually regulation and enforcement. This all takes years. This is the normal course of new regulations.

通常，某项新技术会造成损害或死亡。然后会引发抗议。接着会有调查。几年过去了。然后成立洞察委员会。然后开始制定规则。然后有监督，最终是监管和执法。这一切都需要多年。这是新法规的正常流程。

Look at automotive regulations. The auto industry successfully fought any regulations on seat belts for more than a decade, even though the numbers were extremely obvious. If you had a seat belt on, you were far less likely to die or be seriously injured. Unequivocally. Eventually, after many people died for years, regulators insisted on seat belts.

看看汽车法规。汽车行业成功抵制了安全带的任何法规超过十年，尽管数据非常明显。如果系了安全带，死亡或重伤的可能性大大降低。毫无疑问。最终，在许多人多年死亡后，监管机构坚持要求使用安全带。

This time frame is not relevant to artificial intelligence. You can’t take ten years from the point it becomes dangerous. It’s too late.857

这个时间框架不适用于人工智能。你不能等到它变得危险后再花十年时间。那时就太晚了。857

I was trying to sound the alarm on the AI front for quite a while, but it was clearly having no impact.

我曾试图多次为人工智能敲响警钟，但显然没有任何影响。

I realized we couldn’t stop it, so we’ll have to try to develop it in a good way.858

我意识到我们无法阻止它，所以我们必须尝试以良好的方式开发它。858

This is what we are addressing at Neuralink. Our initial goal is to help people who are quadriplegics or tetraplegics be able to operate their phone or computer. There is always some risk in the beginning, because it’s new technology. The risk–reward trade has to make sense. If you’re quadriplegic and with the Neuralink you can operate a phone even faster than someone with working thumbs, that would be a life-changer.859

这就是我们在Neuralink正在解决的问题。我们的初衷是帮助四肢瘫痪或四肢麻痹的人能够操作手机或电脑。刚开始总有风险，因为这是新技术。风险与收益的权衡必须合理。如果你是四肢瘫痪，通过Neuralink操作手机甚至比有正常拇指的人还快，那将改变生活。859

There are a bunch of other things that could be addressed, like extreme depression or morbid obesity, where people die at age thirty-five. We could literally change the setting in your brain and turn off hunger.860

还有许多其他问题可以解决，比如严重抑郁症或病态肥胖，这些人通常三十五岁就会死亡。我们实际上可以改变你大脑中的设定，关闭饥饿感。860

Memory enhancement can help people who have memory problems, which could allow them to function well much later in life. Mental disablement of one kind or another happens to all of us if we get old enough.861

记忆增强可以帮助有记忆问题的人，使他们能在更晚年仍然良好地生活。随着年龄增长，我们都会经历某种程度的精神障碍。861

Brain–computer interfaces are stunning technology. There may be a way to take the signals from the motor cortex in the brain and send signals to the spine past where the neurons are broken. If so, paralyzed people could move their bodies again.

脑机接口是令人惊叹的技术。可能有方法从大脑运动皮层获取信号，并将信号传递到脊髓，越过受损的神经元。如果成功，瘫痪的人可以重新移动身体。

We may enable people to walk again, which would be wild. Even full-body reanimation. Jesus-level stuff.862

我们可能让人们重新行走，这将是疯狂的。甚至全身复活。堪比耶稣的奇迹。862

POPULATION COLLAPSE人口崩溃

A low birth rate is a slow death for a civilization.863

低出生率是文明的缓慢死亡。863

Most historians overlook the role of low birth rates in the decline of civilizations.864 Will Durant has it correct in The Story of Civilization. A counterintuitive thing happens when civilizations are prosperous for too long—the birth rate declines.865

大多数历史学家忽视了低出生率在文明衰落中的作用。864威尔·杜兰特在《文明的故事》中说得很对。当文明长期繁荣时，会发生一个违反直觉的现象——出生率下降。865

The same thing happened in ancient Rome. Julius Caesar and Augustus saw the problem but couldn’t solve it. Rome fell because the Romans stopped making Romans. That was the fundamental issue.

古罗马也发生了同样的情况。尤利乌斯·凯撒和奥古斯都看到了这个问题，但无法解决。罗马的衰落是因为罗马人不再生育罗马人。这是根本问题。

Yes, there were other causes. They had a series of malaria epidemics, plagues, and whatnot. But they had those before, too. It became fatal when the birth rate was far lower than the death rate.866

是的，还有其他原因。他们经历了一系列疟疾流行、瘟疫等等。但这些以前也有过。致命的是当出生率远低于死亡率时。866

If the birth rate is below the replacement rate and that trend continues, we will eventually disappear. It’s elementary.

如果出生率低于更替率且这一趋势持续，我们最终将消失。这是基本常识。

At a base level: no humans, no humanity.867

最根本的是：没有人类，就没有人性。867

Durant looked at one civilization after another, hundreds of them. They all went through the same cycle. When the civilization was under stress, the birth rate was high. But as soon as there were no external enemies or they had an extended period of prosperity, the birth rate inevitably dropped. Every time. I don’t believe there’s a single exception.868

杜兰特考察了数百个文明。它们都经历了同样的周期。当文明处于压力之下时，出生率很高。但一旦没有外敌或经历了长时间的繁荣，出生率必然下降。每次都是如此。我不相信有任何例外。868

Population collapse is a real and immediate concern.869

人口崩溃是一个真实且紧迫的问题。869

Twenty years ago, I noticed birth rates were trending toward being below replacement level in every wealthy country. We always have to be cautious about extrapolating any demographic curve…but if you extrapolate the curves, if these trends continue, these countries will dwindle into insignificance. They might completely die out.870

二十年前，我注意到所有富裕国家的出生率都趋向于低于更替水平。我们总得谨慎外推任何人口曲线……但如果外推这些曲线，如果这些趋势持续，这些国家将逐渐变得无足轻重。它们甚至可能完全消失。870

It’s easy to predict what the population of any given country will be. Just take how many babies were born, multiply that by life expectancy, and that’s what the population will be if the birth rate continues to that level. It’s very straightforward.871

预测任何国家的人口很简单。只需将出生婴儿数乘以预期寿命，如果出生率保持在该水平，这就是人口数。非常直接。871

You can see the demographic trends clearly. It’s a very slow-moving ship. You know who’s going to be an adult in twenty years based on who was born last year.872

你可以清楚地看到人口趋势。这是一艘缓慢行驶的船。你可以根据去年出生的人，知道二十年后谁将成年。872

China had a one-child policy. About 2015 they changed it to a two-child policy, then a few years later they changed it to a three-child policy. The birth rate kept plummeting the whole time. They recently had their lowest growth rate ever. China’s birth rate reached 40 percent below replacement. Japan is far along in this. Japan declined by six hundred thousand people in 2021.873

中国曾实行一孩政策。大约2015年改为二孩政策，几年后又改为三孩政策。出生率却一直在下降。最近中国的增长率达到了历史最低。中国的出生率比更替率低40%。日本在这方面走得更远。2021年日本人口减少了60万人。873

The US has been below replacement rates since the early 1970s. The only reasons the population is increasing are immigration and people living longer. People living longer is why the population of Earth isn’t plummeting already—but it will.

美国自1970年代初以来一直低于更替率。人口增长的唯一原因是移民和人们寿命延长。寿命延长是地球人口尚未暴跌的原因——但终将暴跌。

Q: There seem to be a lot of people concerned about overpopulation. Isn’t that more of a problem than underpopulation?

问：似乎很多人担心人口过剩。难道这不是比人口不足更大的问题吗？

The biggest myth that exists right now is this “overpopulation” myth.

目前最大的谬论就是“人口过剩”神话。

In fact, we have a population collapse problem.874

事实上，我们面临的是人口崩溃问题。874

They are operating on beliefs that were true in the past but are no longer true.875 These are not subjective matters; you can look at the birth rate. It’s an objective number. How many babies were born? There are obvious public records of this information.876

他们基于过去真实但现在已不再真实的信念行事。875这不是主观问题；你可以查看出生率。这是一个客观数字。出生了多少婴儿？有明显的公共记录。876

I’m pro-environment, but the environmental movement has gone too far.877 The extremist environmentalists view humans as a blight on Earth.878

我支持环保，但环保运动已经走得太远了。877极端环保主义者视人类为地球的祸害。878

Environmentalism started out with good intentions but paved the road to hell. It started to view humans as bad. As a burden on the earth that it couldn’t sustain. These views are completely false.879 Most people in the world are operating under the false impression there are too many people. This is not true. They think that having kids is bad for the environment. It is not.880

环保主义起初出于良好意图，却铺就了通往地狱的道路。它开始将人类视为坏的，是地球无法承受的负担。这些观点完全错误。879世界上大多数人错误地认为人口过多。这不是真的。他们认为生孩子对环境有害，事实并非如此。880

Earth could sustain a human population ten times the current level.881 Do a first-principles analysis. How much land area do we need to grow food? How much would that encroach on natural habitats? What’s the actual food growing potential? Is there enough water? There’s plenty of water because Earth is mostly water—70 percent water by surface area. Desalinization is very inexpensive. There’s not a shortage of water, surface area, or energy to grow food.882

地球可以承载十倍于当前的人口。881做一个第一性原理分析。我们需要多少土地种植食物？这会侵占多少自然栖息地？实际的食物生产潜力是多少？水资源够吗？水资源充足，因为地球表面70%是水。海水淡化成本很低。水资源、土地面积和能源都足够种植食物。882

We need to revive the idea of having children as a social duty; otherwise civilization will just die.883 I keep banging on the baby drum because declining birth rates have been the source of civilizational collapse over and over again throughout history. Let’s try to get this one right.884

我们需要恢复生育作为社会责任的观念，否则文明将灭亡。883我一直强调生育率下降是历史上文明崩溃的根源。让我们这次做对。884

I’ve heard people say many times, “How can I bring a child into this terrible world?”

我听过很多人说，“我怎么能把孩子带到这个糟糕的世界？”

I’m like, “Have you read any history?! Because this is a very good time. Things were way worse at every other time, ever.”885

我会说，“你读过历史吗？！因为现在是非常好的时代。其他任何时候都糟糕得多。”885

Having children is the most optimistic thing somebody could do. It means you care about and believe in the future.886 I always encourage my friends to have kids, and I’m happy to see many of them do.887 Afterward, they thank me. Not one person has said they regret it. Ever.888

生孩子是最乐观的行为。这意味着你关心并相信未来。886我总是鼓励朋友生孩子，很高兴看到他们中的许多人都这么做了。887之后，他们感谢我。没有一个人说后悔。永远没有。888

We are biologically inclined to love and nurture our children. To find it deeply fulfilling. If we weren’t, we would have ceased to exist long ago. Take a wolf or some other aggressive creature. When that creature has babies, the mother nurtures them and is tender and caring. We’ve all evolved to love our offspring. It’s a natural thing. And it’s a relatively fun thing to do!889

我们天生倾向于爱护和养育孩子，感到深深的满足。如果不是这样，我们早就灭绝了。看看狼或其他凶猛动物。当它们有了幼崽，母亲会细心照料，温柔关爱。我们都进化出爱护后代的本能。这是自然且相对愉快的事情！889

I had my first kid when I was twenty-nine. As of now, I have twelve kids with three women. Trying to set a good example here—having a lot of kids.890 I get more joy from my kids than anything else in my life. I’m not saying that’s the main reason to have kids, we should have them anyway, but my kids are certainly the greatest source of joy in my life.891

我29岁时有了第一个孩子。到目前为止，我有12个孩子，三个女人。努力树立好榜样——多生孩子。890我的孩子带给我的快乐超过生活中的任何事。我不是说这是生孩子的主要理由，我们无论如何都应该生，但我的孩子无疑是我生活中最大的快乐源泉。891

We should increase the population of Earth, not decrease it.892

我们应该增加地球人口，而不是减少。892

Go forth and multiply.893

去吧，生养众多。893

ASTEROIDS AND COMETS小行星和彗星

A big rock will hit Earth eventually. We currently have no defense.894

一块大岩石最终会撞击地球。我们目前没有任何防御手段。894

If you think truly long term, you realize eventually there will be some natural disaster—even if it is not made by humans—that destroys all life on Earth.895

如果你真正从长远角度考虑，你会意识到最终会有某种自然灾害——即使不是人为造成的——摧毁地球上的所有生命。895

Most people are probably aware of Halley’s Comet. Slightly under once per century, Halley’s Comet comes near us. There are probably many objects, many comets that have long periods we simply don’t know exist. There are billions, maybe trillions of objects in the outer solar system. Some of them are super-long-period comets.

大多数人可能都知道哈雷彗星。大约每个世纪不到一次，哈雷彗星会靠近我们。可能还有许多我们根本不知道存在的长周期彗星。外太阳系中有数十亿，甚至数万亿个天体，其中一些是超长周期彗星。

For example, when the comet Shoemaker-Levy hit Jupiter, it made a hole in Jupiter the size of Earth. If that hits Earth, that is game over. Dead. Everything is dead.896

例如，当彗星舒梅克-列维撞击木星时，在木星上留下了一个地球大小的洞。如果那样的彗星撞击地球，游戏就结束了。死亡。所有生命都将灭绝。896

For all of history, Earth did not have any ability to stop asteroids. Now with Starship we have some ability to stop small asteroids. Large asteroids and comets are still a danger.

在整个人类历史中，地球都没有能力阻止小行星。现在有了星舰，我们对小型小行星有了一定的防御能力。大型小行星和彗星仍然是威胁。

There’s always some risk of this occurring. The probable lifespan of all forms of life is much greater if we’re a multiplanet and ultimately a multistellar civilization.897

这种事件总是存在一定风险。如果我们成为多行星，最终成为多恒星文明，各种生命形式的可能寿命将大大延长。897

BECOMING MULTIPLANETARY成为多行星物种

The window of opportunity is open right now to make life multiplanetary.

让生命成为多行星物种的机会之窗正敞开着。

We cannot count on it being open for a long time.

我们不能指望这个机会之窗会长时间敞开。

We need to take advantage while this window is open.898

我们需要趁此窗口敞开时抓住机会。898

BECOMING MULTIPLANETARY IS AN EVOLUTIONARY-SCALE EVENT成为多行星物种是一场进化规模的事件

Deciding what is important through the lens of history is a good way to distinguish what seems important now from what is truly important over the long term.899

通过历史的视角来决定什么是重要的，是区分现在看似重要和长期真正重要的好方法。899

If we get to Mars and beyond, that will seem far more important in historical context than anything else we do. Things like the Soviet Union will be forgotten or merely remembered by arcane historical scholars. The invasion of Iraq won’t even be a footnote.900

如果我们到达火星及更远的地方，从历史的角度看，这将比我们做的任何其他事情都重要得多。像苏联这样的事情将被遗忘，或者仅被晦涩的历史学者记住。伊拉克入侵甚至不会成为脚注。900

If you zoom out and look at a long period of time—a four-billion-year history of Earth and the evolution of life itself—there are only about six major milestones. Single-celled life, multicellular life, the differentiation into plants and animals, the transition from ocean creatures to land mammals, and consciousness.

如果你放大视角，观察地球及生命进化的四十亿年历史，只有大约六个重大里程碑。单细胞生命、多细胞生命、植物和动物的分化、从海洋生物到陆地哺乳动物的转变，以及意识的出现。

Life becoming multiplanetary also belongs on this list of milestones. It would be at least as important as life going from the oceans to land, probably more significant.901

生命成为多行星物种也属于这个里程碑列表。它至少和生命从海洋走向陆地一样重要，甚至可能更为重大。901

The universe appears to be 13.8 billion years old. Earth is about 4.5 billion years old. In another 0.5 billion years the sun will expand and make life impossible on Earth. If it had taken consciousness 10 percent longer to evolve, it would never have evolved at all. Just 10 percent longer.

宇宙的年龄约为138亿年。地球约有45亿年历史。再过5亿年，太阳将膨胀，使地球上的生命变得不可能。如果意识进化晚了10%，它可能根本不会进化出来。仅仅晚了10%。

Any species that does not become multiplanetary is simply waiting around until their extinction event, either self-inflicted or external.902

任何不成为多行星物种的物种，实际上就是在等待灭绝事件的到来，无论是自我造成的还是外部的。902

The geological history of Earth is long and complicated. There have been many extinction events, not just a few. Read about the great extinction events in the fossil record. If you do, you’ll see there have been five major extinctions where 80 to 90 percent of all creatures on Earth died.

地球的地质历史漫长且复杂。灭绝事件不止几次。阅读化石记录中的大灭绝事件，你会发现有五次主要灭绝事件，导致地球上80%到90%的生物死亡。

In the Permian Extinction, 90 to 95 percent of all species were destroyed. Frankly, that doesn’t tell the whole story because most of what remained were sponges, fungi, and things like that. Unless you were a mushroom or a cockroach, you died. Almost no large life survived the Permian Extinction.903

在二叠纪灭绝事件中，90%到95%的物种被毁灭。坦率地说，这还不是全部，因为幸存的大多是海绵、真菌之类的生物。除非你是蘑菇或蟑螂，否则你就死了。几乎没有大型生命体幸存下来。903

This doesn’t count the many cases where entire continents were destroyed. That happened a lot, but wouldn’t count as a major extinction. Yellowstone erupts every seven hundred thousand years or so. That would destroy pretty much all life in North America. At least we’ll see that one coming now, thanks to our current technology.904

这还不包括许多整个大陆被毁灭的情况。那种情况发生过很多次，但不算作重大灭绝事件。黄石公园大约每七十万年喷发一次。那将摧毁北美几乎所有生命。至少现在凭借我们的技术，我们能预见到那次喷发。904

Humans can cause our own extinction too; other creatures didn’t have that option.905

人类也可能导致自身灭绝；其他生物没有这种选择。905

I’m a fan of Carl Sagan. He had a great way of putting things: “All of our consciousness, all our civilizations, everything we’ve ever known and done is on this one tiny blue dot.”

我是卡尔·萨根的粉丝。他有一句很棒的话：“我们所有的意识、所有文明、我们曾经知道和做过的一切，都在这一个微小的蓝点上。”

People get too trapped in their squabbles among humans. They don’t think of the big picture. They take civilization and our continued existence for granted. We shouldn’t do that. Look at the history of civilizations: they rise and they fall.

人们太容易陷入人与人之间的争吵，忽视了大局。他们把文明和我们持续存在视为理所当然。我们不应该这样。看看文明的历史：它们兴起又衰落。

Now there’s no geographic isolation. Our civilization is globalized, so civilization rises and falls together. This is a big risk. This should be the most important lesson of history: Things don’t always go up.906

现在没有地理隔离。我们的文明是全球化的，所以文明兴衰是同步的。这是一个巨大风险。这应该是历史最重要的教训：事情并不总是向上发展。906

I’m fairly optimistic about the future of Earth. I don’t want people to have the wrong impression, like we’re all about to die. Things will most likely be okay for a long time on Earth. Most likely, but nothing is certain.907

我对地球的未来相当乐观。我不想让人们误以为我们都快死了。地球上的情况很可能会持续良好很长时间。很可能，但没有什么是确定的。907

When I talked to Stephen Hawking many years ago, he thought there was roughly a 1 percent chance of civilization ending in any given century. Even a 1 percent chance of consciousness ending is still too much. It’s worth spending a fair bit of effort to ensure we back up the biosphere, or build planetary redundancy. This is important. It’s like Russian roulette where ninety-nine barrels are empty. Every century is a click. Click, click, click. Eventually…908

多年前我和斯蒂芬·霍金交谈时，他认为文明在任何一个世纪结束的概率大约是1%。即使意识终结的概率只有1%，也太高了。值得花费相当多的努力来备份生物圈，或建立行星冗余。这很重要。这就像俄罗斯轮盘，99个弹膛是空的。每个世纪都是一次扣动扳机。咔嗒，咔嗒，咔嗒。最终……908

Making life multiplanetary is one of the most important things we could accomplish. This will help preserve the light of consciousness.

让生命成为多行星物种是我们能完成的最重要的事情之一。这将帮助保护意识之光。

The probability of consciousness existing for a long time becomes much greater if we’re on two independent planets. If something catastrophic happened to Earth, life would still exist on another planet.909

如果我们存在于两个独立的星球上，意识长期存在的概率会大大增加。如果地球发生灾难，生命仍将在另一个星球上存在。909

Say there was a giant meteor impact, a supervolcano, a massive nuclear war, or some super virus. It might not destroy human civilization, but it could knock us back to a much lower technology level, and then we risk a decline to our extinction.910

假设发生了巨型陨石撞击、超级火山爆发、大规模核战争或超级病毒。这可能不会摧毁人类文明，但可能会将我们技术水平降到很低，然后我们面临衰退直至灭绝的风险。910

There are certain things we simply cannot avoid on Earth. Is it within your power or mine to stop World War III? I don’t think so.911

地球上有些事情我们无法避免。阻止第三次世界大战在你我能力范围内吗？我认为不是。911

We may get hit by a comet like the dinosaurs. If the dinosaurs had spaceships, they’d probably still be around. Even if we dodge all of that, the sun will continue to expand and eventually engulf Earth and destroy all life in the solar system.912

我们可能会像恐龙一样被彗星撞击。如果恐龙有宇宙飞船，它们可能还活着。即使我们躲过了这些，太阳仍会继续膨胀，最终吞没地球，摧毁太阳系内所有生命。912

There’s a pretty substantial bifurcation in our future: We’re either out there among the stars on many planets or confined to Earth until some eventual extinction.913

我们的未来有一个相当明显的分叉：要么我们在众多星球间的星辰中生存，要么被限制在地球上直到最终灭绝。913

This is the first moment in 4.5 billion years that it has been possible to extend life beyond Earth.914

这是45亿年来第一次有可能将生命延伸到地球之外。914

IF YOU LOVE LIFE, PROTECT IT如果你热爱生命，就保护它

I’ve read a lot of history, including the darkest, worst parts of it. Despite all that, I still love humanity. This is why I care about us becoming a multiplanet species, a spacefaring civilization—because I love humanity. I wish to see it prosper and be happy.915

我读过很多历史，包括其中最黑暗、最糟糕的部分。尽管如此，我依然热爱人类。这就是为什么我关心我们成为多星球物种、成为一个太空文明——因为我热爱人类。我希望看到人类繁荣幸福。915

I’m troubled by the question, “Why have we not seen any aliens?” It could be because intelligence, or even just life, is incredibly rare. Maybe we’re the only life in this galaxy. We should do everything possible to ensure this tiny candle of consciousness does not go out.916

“为什么我们没有见过外星人？”这个问题让我很困扰。可能是因为智慧生命，甚至生命本身极其罕见。也许我们是这个银河系中唯一的生命。我们应该尽一切可能确保这微弱的意识之光不熄灭。916

Life on Earth hasn’t been around very long, and it can easily disappear. Clearly, we need to extend life beyond Earth and become a multiplanet species.917

地球上的生命存在时间并不长，而且很容易消失。显然，我们需要将生命延伸到地球之外，成为多星球物种。917

If we make life multiplanetary, there may come a day when some plants and animals die out on Earth but are still alive on Mars.918

“如果我们让生命成为多星球的，未来某一天地球上一些植物和动物可能灭绝，但它们仍然活在火星上。918”

Q: Wouldn’t it be better to use all these resources and try to resolve problems on Earth?919

问：用所有这些资源来解决地球上的问题不是更好吗？919

About half my money is dedicated to help solve problems like climate change on Earth and half to help establish a self-sustaining city on Mars to ensure the continuation of life of all species.920

我大约一半的资金用于帮助解决地球上的气候变化等问题，另一半用于帮助建立火星上的自给自足城市，以确保所有物种生命的延续。920

The point is not to move from Earth to another planet and let Earth die. That’s not what I’m saying at all.921

重点不是从地球搬到另一个星球然后让地球死去。我完全不是这个意思。921

To put this into perspective, the amount of resources I’m talking about for making life multiplanetary would be much less than 1 percent of all resources on Earth.922

换个角度看，我所说的让生命成为多星球所需的资源，远不到地球上所有资源的1%。922

Think of it as resource allocation. Do you think it’s worth spending half a percent of Earth’s resources to ensure we extend consciousness to Mars and other planets, so that no single event can be the end of our civilization?923

把它看作资源分配。你认为花费地球资源的半个百分点来确保我们将意识延伸到火星和其他星球，避免任何单一事件终结我们的文明，值得吗？923

Redundancy of life is the defensive reason to go to Mars. But also, going to Mars is the grandest adventure I could possibly imagine. I can’t think of anything more exciting, more fun, and more inspiring for the future than to have a base on Mars.

生命的冗余是前往火星的防御理由。但同时，去火星是我能想象的最宏伟的冒险。我想不到比在火星建立基地更令人兴奋、更有趣、更鼓舞人心的未来了。

Great and terrible things will happen along the way, just as they happened in the creation of the United States. It will be difficult, and some people will probably die. But it will be incredibly inspiring.

在这个过程中会发生伟大而艰难的事情，就像美国建国时一样。过程会很艰难，可能有人会牺牲。但这将极具鼓舞人心。

Ultimately, if you care about life on Earth, you should want that life to become multiplanetary and ultimately multistellar. Otherwise you’re signing the death warrant for all life as we know it. It’s inevitable.924

归根结底，如果你关心地球上的生命，你应该希望生命成为多星球的，最终成为多恒星的。否则，你就是在为我们所知的所有生命签下死亡判决。这是不可避免的。924

Think of us being a multiplanet species as taking out insurance for life itself.

把我们成为多星球物种看作是为生命本身购买保险。

Life insurance, for life.925

生命的保险。925

THE GATEWAY TO MARS通往火星之门

What started off as basically a sandbar in the middle of nowhere is now Starbase, Texas. We named it “The Gateway to Mars,” because this is where we’re going to develop the technology necessary to take humanity, civilization, and life as we know it to another planet for the first time in the 4.5-billion-year history of Earth.926

最初不过是荒无人烟中的一条沙洲，如今这里已成为德克萨斯的星港。我们称它为“通往火星之门”，因为这里将是我们开发将人类、文明和生命首次带到另一颗行星所需技术的地方，这是地球45亿年历史上的第一次。926

Q: Why Mars?927

问：为什么选择火星？927

There are not a lot of options, frankly. Venus is a superheated, high-pressure acid bath. Venus is hell, almost literally.928 The moon is closer but it doesn’t have an atmosphere, has only one-sixth of Earth’s gravity, and is missing many key resources.929 Plus, a Mars base is more likely to survive a conflict on Earth than a moon base.930

坦白说，选择不多。金星是一个超高温、高压的酸性浴池。金星几乎就是地狱。928月球虽然更近，但没有大气层，只有地球重力的六分之一，且缺少许多关键资源。929此外，火星基地比月球基地更有可能在地球发生冲突时幸存下来。930

We could establish a crude permanent base on the moon. That would be the next step up from Apollo. Let’s not just go there for a few hours and head back; let’s have an occupied science base on the moon. We could build epic telescopes on the moon. They would enable us to see what’s going on out there. Maybe we would detect those aliens!931

我们可以在月球上建立一个粗糙的永久基地。这将是阿波罗计划之后的下一步。我们不仅仅是去月球待几个小时然后返回，而是在月球上建立一个有人科学基地。我们可以在月球上建造宏伟的望远镜，帮助我们观察宇宙中的动向。也许我们能探测到那些外星人！931

When we’re out there exploring the galaxy, we might find a whole bunch of dead one-planet civilizations. They just never made it to the next planet. Can you imagine doing archaeology on strange ghost-town planets?!932

当我们在银河系中探索时，可能会发现许多死去的单星球文明。他们从未成功迈向下一颗行星。你能想象在奇异的鬼城星球上做考古吗？！932

Progress is measured by the timeline to establish a self-sustaining civilization on Mars. That’s how we gauge our progress at Starbase.933

进展的衡量标准是建立火星自给自足文明的时间表。这就是我们在星港衡量进展的方式。933

Occupy Mars, man.934

“占领火星，伙计。”934

Q: How can we actually get enough stuff to Mars to build a city?

问：我们如何实际将足够的物资运送到火星去建造城市？

We’re now at the point where we can produce a Starship roughly every two or three weeks. Now, we don’t always produce a ship every two or three weeks because we are still making design upgrades. But ultimately we’re aiming for the ability to produce a thousand ships a year.935

我们现在已经能大约每两到三周生产一艘星舰。虽然我们并不总是每两三周就生产一艘，因为还在进行设计升级，但最终目标是每年生产一千艘。935

Starbase will probably be making as many Starships for Mars as Boeing and Airbus make commercial airplanes each year. This is really an enormous scale of manufacturing, because each Starship is bigger than a 747 or an A380.

星港制造的火星星舰数量可能会和波音、空客每年制造的商用飞机数量相当。这是一个极其庞大的制造规模，因为每艘星舰都比747或A380还要大。

Tesla and other car companies are still building far higher volumes of complex manufactured tonnage than SpaceX. That is a way of saying it’s achievable. These numbers are insanely high by traditional space standards but they are achievable, because they have been achieved in other industries.936

特斯拉和其他汽车公司制造的复杂制造吨位仍远高于SpaceX。这说明这是可实现的。按传统航天标准，这些数字极其庞大，但在其他行业已经实现过，所以是可行的。936

Starship is the key to preserving the light of consciousness. That’s what it’s all about.

“星舰是守护意识之光的关键。这就是它的全部意义所在。”

It may end up being the most important thing that we ever do.937

“它可能成为我们所做过的最重要的事情。”937

We’re hoping to increase the cadence of flights to Mars dramatically with every launch window. Roughly every two years we will dramatically increase the number of ships that go to Mars. Ultimately we will try to get to one thousand or two thousand ships per Mars rendezvous.938

我们希望每个发射窗口都能大幅提升飞往火星的飞行频率。大约每两年，我们将大幅增加前往火星的星舰数量。最终目标是在每次火星会合时达到一千到两千艘星舰。938

We’ll need to do a lot of orbital refueling. Orbiting rocket tanker ships will replenish the propellant of the ships that will actually go to Mars. We’re aiming to demonstrate ship-to-ship propellant transfer. It’s hard to make this not look a bit naughty because it’s two ships connecting and doing a fluid transfer.

我们需要大量轨道加注燃料。轨道上的火箭加油船将为实际飞往火星的飞船补充推进剂。我们计划演示船对船的推进剂转移。虽然这看起来有点调皮，因为是两艘飞船连接并进行流体转移。

We would create a propellant depot ship that would look like a huge hot dog. Shortly before going to Mars, Starships would take off with a couple hundred tons of payload from Earth. They would reach orbit with almost no propellant left, then get refilled by the tanker for the journey to Mars.939

我们将制造一个推进剂补给船，看起来像一个巨大的热狗。在飞往火星前，星舰将从地球带着几百吨有效载荷起飞，进入轨道时几乎没有推进剂，然后由加油船补充燃料，准备前往火星。939

We need roughly ten thousand missions to get to a million tons transferred to Mars. We think we can do this by 2044.940 My guess is that it will require about a million tons to build a self-sufficient city, but it might be ten million tons. I hope it’s not one hundred million tons. That’d be a lot.941

我们大约需要一万次任务才能将一百万吨物资运送到火星。我们预计2044年前可以实现这一目标。940我估计建造一个自给自足的城市大约需要一百万吨物资，但也可能是一千万吨。希望不会达到一亿吨，那就太多了。941

Starship in its final form will probably do well over two hundred tons to orbit each flight, with full reusability and the ability to fly multiple times a day. We plan to launch about five uncrewed Starships to Mars in 2026. If those all land safely, then crewed missions are possible in 2028. If we encounter challenges, the crewed missions will be postponed two more years.

最终形态的星舰每次飞行可能将超过两百吨送入轨道，具备完全重复使用能力和每天多次飞行的能力。我们计划在2026年发射约五艘无人星舰前往火星。如果它们全部安全着陆，载人任务可能在2028年实现。如果遇到挑战，载人任务将推迟两年。

It is only possible to travel from Earth to Mars every two years when the planets are aligned. If Mars is on the other side of the sun, you can’t get there. Can’t go through the sun.942 This increases the difficulty but also serves to immunize Mars from many catastrophic events on Earth.

只有当地球和火星行星排列合适时，才可能每两年进行一次地球到火星的旅行。如果火星在太阳的另一侧，是无法到达的。不能穿越太阳。942这增加了难度，但也使火星免受地球许多灾难性事件的影响。

SpaceX will increase the number of spaceships traveling to Mars exponentially with every opportunity. We want to enable anyone who wants to be a space traveler and go to Mars. That means you, your family, or friends—anyone who dreams of great adventure.943

SpaceX将利用每次机会指数级增加前往火星的飞船数量。我们希望让任何想成为太空旅行者并前往火星的人都能实现。这意味着你、你的家人或朋友——任何梦想伟大冒险的人。943

We want to get to the point where we’re sending over a million tons at every Mars transfer window. Then we’re a serious civilization. A megaton per transfer window. We can’t fly there continuously, so we’d have a gathering of a thousand ships or more.944

我们希望达到每个火星转移窗口运送超过一百万吨物资的水平。那时我们就是一个真正的文明。每个转移窗口百万吨。我们不能连续飞行，所以会有一千艘或更多飞船聚集。944

Eventually, there will be thousands of Starships going to Mars. Imagine an armada of Starships waiting in orbit for the planets to align and then this gigantic Starfleet taking off for Mars.945 It will be a glorious sight to see! Can you imagine?946

最终，将有数千艘星舰飞往火星。想象一下，一支星舰舰队在轨道上等待行星排列，然后这支庞大的星际舰队起航前往火星。945那将是一幅壮丽的景象！你能想象吗？946

This is an extremely difficult engineering problem. But no new physics is required.947

“这是一个极其困难的工程问题。但不需要新的物理学。”947

Q: What’s the math on the journey to Mars?

问：前往火星的数学计算是怎样的？

The fundamental optimization is minimizing cost per ton to orbit, and ultimately cost per ton to the surface of Mars. This may seem like a purely mercantile objective, but it is actually the thing that needs to be optimized. There is a certain cost per ton to the surface of Mars where we can afford to establish a self-sustaining city. If we don’t get the cost down, we can’t afford to do it.

根本的优化目标是最小化每吨送入轨道的成本，最终是每吨送到火星表面的成本。这看似纯粹是商业目标，但实际上这是必须优化的关键。有一个每吨送到火星表面的成本阈值，低于它我们才能负担得起建立自给自足的城市。如果成本降不下来，我们就做不到。

It will cost maybe a quarter of a percent or half a percent of gross domestic product (GDP). That is palatable. A few people going to Mars will not cause some meaningful drop in the standard of living.948 For less than 1 percent of GDP, we can buy life insurance.949

这可能花费国内生产总值（GDP）的四分之一到半个百分点。这是可以接受的。少数人前往火星不会导致生活水平显著下降。948用不到1%的GDP，我们就能买到生命保险。949

Starlink internet is what’s being used to pay for humanity getting to Mars. I would like to thank everyone who has bought Starlink because you’re helping secure the future of civilization.

“星链互联网正在资助人类前往火星。我想感谢所有购买星链的人，因为你们正在帮助保障文明的未来。”

Thank you.950

“谢谢你们。950”

Right now, you couldn’t fly to Mars for a trillion dollars. No amount of money could get any human a ticket to Mars. First, we need to make this possible. But we don’t just want to have flags and footprints on Mars for fifty years, like we did with the moon. To pass the great filter of existing only on one planet, we need to truly become a multiplanet species.951

目前，即使花费一万亿美元，也无法买到飞往火星的机票。没有多少钱能让任何人类获得火星之旅的资格。首先，我们需要让这成为可能。但我们不想像对待月球那样，只留下旗帜和脚印五十年。为了通过“伟大过滤器”，摆脱只存在于一颗行星的命运，我们必须真正成为多星球物种。951

BUILDING THE NEW WORLD建设新世界

Attendance is not mandatory here. It’ll be dangerous and people might die.

这里不强制出席。会很危险，可能有人会死。

Few people will want to go to Mars in the beginning. But for some, the excitement of the frontier exceeds the concern of danger.952 I think it would be the adventure—the best adventure one could possibly go on—to go build a new civilization on a new planet.953

一开始很少有人愿意去火星。但对一些人来说，边疆的刺激超过了对危险的担忧。952 我认为这将是一场冒险——可能是人们能经历的最棒的冒险——去新星球上建设一个新的文明。953

Some people seem to think this will be an escape hatch, some luxury resort for rich people. It is not. It’s a high probability of death relative to Earth. Long journey. Not great food. A lot of hard work.954

有些人似乎认为这将是一个逃生出口，是富人的豪华度假胜地。事实并非如此。相较于地球，死亡概率很高。旅程漫长。食物不好吃。工作非常辛苦。954

You have to spend six months on a rocket with a hundred other people. It will be cramped. Like old ocean voyages, six months packed on a little ship in the middle of the ocean.955

你必须和一百个其他人一起在火箭上度过六个月。空间会很拥挤。就像古老的海洋航行，六个月挤在一艘小船上，漂泊在大海中央。955

It’s going to take a while to build a real civilization on Mars. The critical threshold is if the ships from Earth stop coming for any reason, does the Mars City die out or not? That’s a high bar. You can’t be missing anything.956

在火星上建立真正的文明需要一段时间。关键门槛是，如果地球来的飞船因任何原因停止抵达，火星城会不会灭亡？这是一个很高的标准。你不能缺少任何东西。956

Imagine you’re on a long sea voyage and the only thing you’re missing is vitamin C. It’s only a matter of time before it’s curtains. You’ve got to have all the things necessary to sustain civilization on Mars, including about a million people.957

想象你在一次漫长的海上航行中，唯一缺少的是维生素C。迟早会完蛋。你必须拥有维持火星文明所需的一切，包括大约一百万人口。957

I would like to die on Mars…just not on impact.958

我想死在火星上……只是不要死于撞击。958

Some Starships could come back to Earth. We want to offer an option of coming back, but most people who go to Mars will probably never come back to Earth.959

有些星舰可能会返回地球。我们想提供返回的选项，但大多数去火星的人可能永远不会回地球。959

Most of the ships we send will probably stay there, especially while colonizing Mars, because the ship itself will be so valuable there. Martians will take apart the ship and use it for raw materials.960

我们发射的大多数飞船可能会留在那里，尤其是在殖民火星期间，因为飞船本身在那里非常有价值。火星人会拆解飞船，用作原材料。960

Once we get to Mars, there will be a lot to do, and it’ll take a while to build it all. We have to build out the industrial base, then the city. First, we build a giant solar panel farm to generate energy. Then a facility to make fuel, oxygen to grow plants for food, and everything necessary to support life.961

一旦我们到达火星，会有很多事情要做，建设也需要时间。我们必须先建立工业基础，然后建设城市。首先，我们建造一个巨大的太阳能电池板农场来发电。然后建造燃料制造厂、制造氧气以种植食物的植物，以及支持生命所需的一切设施。961

Ultimately we’ll need all these things: power generation, ice mining, mining in general, propellent production, long-duration life support, construction, and global communication.962

最终我们需要所有这些设施：发电、冰矿开采、矿业、推进剂生产、长期生命支持、建筑和全球通信。962

A fuel plant will create the fuel for the return trips of many Starships. It’s mostly going to be oxygen plants, because rockets run on 78 percent liquid oxygen, 22 percent fuel. Mars has a CO2 atmosphere and water ice, which is CO2 plus H2O, so plenty of raw material to make what rockets need: CH4 (methane) and O22 (oxygen). Rocket fuel is easy to create on Mars, and in many other parts of the solar system.963

燃料厂将为许多星舰的返程制造燃料。主要是氧气厂，因为火箭燃料是78%液态氧，22%燃料。火星有二氧化碳大气和水冰，水冰是二氧化碳加水，因此有充足的原料制造火箭所需的甲烷（CH4）和氧气（O2）。火箭燃料在火星以及太阳系许多其他地方都很容易制造。963

In the beginning, people would live in glass domes. Over time, we’d terraform Mars and make it like Earth.964

一开始，人们会住在玻璃穹顶里。随着时间推移，我们会对火星进行地球化改造，使其变得像地球一样。964

Mars is cold, but if we warm it up we’ll get liquid water. Mars would have an ocean roughly a mile deep on 40 percent of the planet once we warm it up. That’s a lot of water. A lot of the ice you see on the poles is actually dry ice. It’s frozen CO2.965

火星很冷，但如果我们加热它，就会有液态水。加热后，火星大约40%的面积将拥有约一英里深的海洋。那是大量的水。你在极地看到的许多冰其实是干冰，是冻结的二氧化碳。965

Terraforming Mars mostly consists of warming it up. We could warm it up either with solar reflectors in orbit or a lot of thermonuclear explosions.966

地球化火星主要是加热它。我们可以用轨道上的太阳反射镜或者大量热核爆炸来加热它。966

It sounds crazy, but a series of thermonuclear explosions basically creates an artificial sun. If you’re worried that it will generate dangerous radiation, have you stood in the sun? The sun is a giant thermonuclear reactor. Obviously we can stand in front of the sun and not die. We could launch a missile every ten seconds or so. It would be like gigantic thermonuclear fireworks.967

听起来疯狂，但一系列热核爆炸基本上会制造一个人造太阳。如果你担心它会产生危险辐射，你站在太阳下试过吗？太阳是一个巨大的热核反应堆。显然我们可以站在太阳前而不死。我们可以每十秒发射一枚导弹。这将像巨大的热核烟花。967

We’d create two little suns, pulsing above the north and south poles.

我们会在南北极上空制造两个小太阳，脉动发光。

That would warm the poles up enough that the frozen carbon dioxide (CO2) would gasify. The Martian atmosphere would get more dense, with water vapor and CO2 in the air. In Mars’s case, more CO2 in the air is good, because it will create a positive cycle to make it warmer with more liquid water—like Earth’s environment.968

这会使极地温度升高，冻结的二氧化碳会气化。火星大气会变得更浓厚，空气中含有水蒸气和二氧化碳。对火星来说，空气中更多的二氧化碳是好事，因为它会形成一个正反馈循环，使温度升高，产生更多液态水——就像地球环境一样。968

Even if you don’t go to Mars, you can watch this all happen on TV. It’ll be so cool.969

即使你不去火星，也可以在电视上观看这一切发生。那会非常酷。969

What I’m describing may sound crazy, but it is a small fraction of what will ultimately be done, as long as we become a two-planet civilization. Look at the history of shipping technology in Europe. When all you had to do was cross the Mediterranean, the ships were pretty lame—they only had short-range vessels, which couldn’t cross the Atlantic.

我描述的可能听起来疯狂，但这只是最终实现的很小一部分，只要我们成为一个双星球文明。看看欧洲航运技术的历史。当时只需要穿越地中海，船只很简陋——只有短程船，无法横渡大西洋。

Without the forcing function of long-range commerce, shipping technology didn’t improve much. You could do mostly the same things with ships around the time of Julius Caesar and the time of Columbus. Fifteen hundred years later, ships could still only cross the Mediterranean. But as soon as there was a reason to cross the Atlantic, shipping technology improved dramatically. The American colonies were needed for that to happen.970

没有长距离贸易的推动，航运技术几乎没有进步。你可以用船做的事情，凯撒时代和哥伦布时代差不多。1500年后，船仍然只能穿越地中海。但一旦有了横渡大西洋的需求，航运技术就大幅提升。美洲殖民地的出现促成了这一切。970

Reusable rockets are the modern-day equivalent of the first ships that could cross oceans.

可重复使用火箭是现代版的第一批能跨洋航行的船只。

Until you have a breakthrough technology enabling travel, there’s no way for entrepreneurial energy to do anything.

除非有突破性技术实现旅行，否则创业热情无法发挥作用。

Once we build reusable rockets, suddenly the opportunity is immense. We’re going to do our best to get you to Mars, and make sure there’s an environment there where entrepreneurs can continue building and flourish.971

一旦我们建成可重复使用火箭，机会就巨大。我们会尽力送你去火星，并确保那里有一个环境，让创业者能持续建设并繁荣发展。971

The same thing happened with the first cross-continental railroad in the United States, the Union Pacific Railroad. When they were building the Union Pacific Railroad, nobody could predict Silicon Valley, Hollywood, or California becoming the most populous state in the country. That would have sounded crazy. But then they discovered gold.972

美国第一条横贯大陆铁路——联合太平洋铁路——也是如此。建造时没人能预见硅谷、好莱坞或加州成为全国人口最多的州。这听起来很疯狂。但后来他们发现了黄金。972

It’s incumbent on SpaceX and other organizations to figure out how to get to Mars. Otherwise, nothing else matters.

SpaceX和其他组织有责任弄清楚如何到达火星。否则，其他一切都无关紧要。

Once we get there, there’s a lot that can be done.973

一旦我们到达那里，有很多事情可以做。973

There will be a lot of super exciting things that are hard to predict. After the basic infrastructure is built on Mars, there will be an explosion of opportunity for entrepreneurs, because our new world will need everything from iron foundries to pizza joints. This includes everything you can imagine, like starting the first Italian restaurant on Mars. That would be cool, and somebody’s gotta do it.974

会有许多令人兴奋且难以预测的事情。在火星基础设施建成后，创业机会将爆发，因为我们的新世界需要从铁厂到披萨店的各种设施。这包括你能想象的一切，比如开设火星上的第一家意大利餐厅。那会很酷，总得有人去做。974

COLONIZING THE GALAXY殖民银河系

Earth is the cradle of humanity. We cannot stay in the cradle forever.

地球是人类的摇篮。我们不能永远停留在摇篮里。

It is time to go forth, be out there among the stars. Expand the scope and scale of human consciousness.975

是时候走出去，去星辰之间。扩展人类意识的范围和规模。975

The big picture isn’t just to back up the hard drive of humanity, but to grow humanity into a true multiplanetary species. Let’s establish a regular cargo route to Mars. With the economic driver of interplanetary commerce, there will be resources and incentives to massively improve space transport technology, and then things will go to a whole new level.976

大局不仅仅是备份人类的硬盘，而是将人类发展成为真正的多行星物种。让我们建立一条定期的火星货运路线。借助星际商业的经济驱动力，将有资源和激励大幅提升太空运输技术，届时一切将迈上全新高度。976

Starship is not suitable for going to another star system, but it is a general solution for transport anywhere inside the solar system. Once we have a propellant depot on Mars, we can travel to the asteroid belt, the moons of Jupiter and Saturn, and ultimately anywhere in the solar system. This is huge.977

星舰不适合前往其他恒星系统，但它是太阳系内任何地方运输的通用解决方案。一旦我们在火星建立了推进剂补给站，就能前往小行星带、木星和土星的卫星，最终抵达太阳系内的任何地方。这意义重大。977

When space travel becomes as common as air travel, the future of civilization will be assured.

当太空旅行变得像航空旅行一样普及时，文明的未来将得到保障。

One way to look at the progress of civilization is the percentage completion of each Kardashev level. Kardashev level one is harnessing all the energy of a planet. In my opinion, we’ve only harnessed maybe 1 or 2 percent of Earth’s energy. So we’ve got a long way to go to complete Kardashev level one.

观察文明进步的一种方式是看每个卡尔达肖夫等级的完成百分比。卡尔达肖夫一级是利用一个行星的全部能量。依我看，我们目前只利用了地球能量的1%或2%。所以距离完成卡尔达肖夫一级还有很长的路要走。

Kardashev level two is complete when we’ve harnessed all the energy of the sun. That would be—I don’t know—a billion times more energy than Earth? Maybe closer to a trillion.

卡尔达肖夫二级是当我们利用了太阳的全部能量时完成的。那将是——我不知道——比地球能量多十亿倍？也许更接近一万亿倍。

Then, Kardashev level three would be all the energy of a galaxy. We’re pretty far from that. So we’re at the very, very early stage of the intelligence big bang.978

然后，卡尔达肖夫三级是利用整个银河系的能量。我们离那还很远。所以我们处于智能大爆炸的非常早期阶段。978

If we can establish a Mars colony, we can almost certainly colonize the whole solar system. We’ll go to the moons of Jupiter, at least some of the outer ones, probably Titan on Saturn, and the asteroids. Once we have an Earth-to-Mars economy as a forcing function, we’ll cover the whole solar system.979

如果我们能建立火星殖民地，我们几乎可以肯定殖民整个太阳系。我们会去木星的卫星，至少是一些外层卫星，可能还有土星的泰坦，以及小行星。一旦我们拥有地球到火星的经济作为推动力，我们将覆盖整个太阳系。979

We have to make Mars work. If we’re going to have any chance of sending stuff to other star systems, we need to be laser-focused on Mars. That’s our next step.980

我们必须让火星成功。如果我们想有机会向其他恒星系统发送物资，就必须全力以赴专注于火星。这是我们的下一步。980

On a galactic time scale, even with slower-than-light travel, we could colonize the whole galaxy. Even some of the neighboring galaxies. If we get a million years, even with no new physics, could we colonize the galaxy? Absolutely. The entire galaxy.981

在银河时间尺度上，即使是亚光速旅行，我们也能殖民整个银河系。甚至包括一些邻近的星系。如果给我们一百万年，即使没有新物理，我们能殖民银河系吗？绝对可以。整个银河系。981

You are the magicians of the twenty-first century; don’t let anything hold you back.

你们是21世纪的魔法师；不要让任何事阻碍你们。

Imagination is the limit.

想象力就是极限。

Go out there and create some magic.982

走出去，创造一些魔法吧。982

这些被选为使埃隆及其公司成功的一些基本理念，经过编辑或改写成简短易记的格言。

尽可能多地接触聪明人。多读书。1015

有许多方式可以更深入地了解埃隆的思想。请访问 ElonMuskBook.org，获取额外章节、有声书、访谈等内容。

You might also appreciate my previous books, The Almanack of Naval Ravikant: A Guide To Wealth and Happiness and The Anthology of Balaji: A Guide to Technology, Truth, and Building the Future.

你可能也会喜欢我之前的著作《纳瓦尔·拉维坎特年鉴：财富与幸福指南》和《巴拉吉选集：技术、真理与未来构建指南》。

If you love the illustrations in this book, created by Jack Butcher, find more of his illustrations and much more of his work at VisualizeValue.com.

如果你喜欢本书中由杰克·布彻创作的插图，可以在 VisualizeValue.com 看到他更多的插图作品及其他创作。

To learn about similar ideas, concepts, technologies, companies, and investments, join my email list at EJorgenson.com and subscribe to the podcast Smart Friends.

想了解类似的理念、概念、技术、公司和投资，欢迎加入我的邮件列表 EJorgenson.com，并订阅播客《聪明朋友》。

There are always more names in the back of the book than there are on the front. Thank you to all who generously gave their time, expertise, wisdom, and skill to create this book.

书的后面总有比封面更多的名字。感谢所有慷慨奉献时间、专业知识、智慧和技能来创作这本书的人。

It’s staggering to think how many talented people left their fingerprints on these pages. I’m grateful to every one of you.

想到这么多才华横溢的人在这些页面上留下了他们的印记，真是令人震惊。我感激你们每一个人。

Thank you first to Elon, for allowing and encouraging this project. I’m honored by the opportunity to build something around your ideas. I appreciate your trust, generosity, and support.

首先感谢埃隆，感谢你允许并鼓励这个项目。我很荣幸有机会围绕你的想法构建一些东西。感谢你的信任、慷慨和支持。

I am grateful to Naval for writing the foreword to this book. You put into words every hope that I had for the impact this book could have, and it meant the world to me. Thank you for kicking off this writing adventure, supporting this book, and being you.

感谢Naval为本书撰写前言。你用语言表达了我对这本书可能产生影响的所有期望，这对我意义重大。感谢你开启这段写作旅程，支持这本书，并做你自己。

I am grateful for a fantastic editor in Rachel Jepsen, and the supportive team at Scribe Media, in particular my publishing manager, Emmy Koziak, for her patience and flexibility.

感谢出色的编辑Rachel Jepsen，以及Scribe Media支持团队，特别是我的出版经理Emmy Koziak，感谢她的耐心和灵活性。

I’m grateful to Dylan Kurt for reaching out and taking on the thousand-and-one tasks that shaped this book and every word in it.

感谢Dylan Kurt主动联系并承担了塑造这本书及其每一个字的千头万绪的任务。

I am grateful to Jack Butcher for lending his immense talents once again. The illustrations and visuals in this book are entirely his. He clarifies and elevates every idea he touches. He’s a living example of these ideals. Thank you for taking this project on in addition to all of your own. I appreciate your talent, kindness, and heart.

感谢Jack Butcher再次贡献他巨大的才华。本书中的插图和视觉效果完全出自他手。他澄清并提升了他所触及的每一个理念。他是这些理想的活生生的例子。感谢你在忙于自己项目的同时承担了这个项目。我感激你的才华、善良和真诚。

I am grateful to Sam Teller, John Durant, and Sky King for their unique shows of faith in this project and me personally. You are deeply appreciated for your generosity and care.

感谢Sam Teller、John Durant和Sky King对这个项目以及我个人表现出的独特信任。你们的慷慨和关怀让我深感感激。

I am grateful to all of the fantastic interviewers and writers who created the building blocks of this book. I learned something from all of you, and I appreciate you creating and sharing excellent work.

感谢所有出色的采访者和作家，他们构建了这本书的基石。我从你们每个人身上学到了东西，感谢你们创作并分享了优秀的作品。

I remain grateful to my parents for every gift, effort, and sacrifice that put me in a position to create this book. You lovingly built the foundation for everything I ever do, and I’ll never forget that.

我永远感激我的父母，感谢他们给予的每一份礼物、努力和牺牲，使我有能力创作这本书。你们充满爱心地为我所做的一切奠定了基础，我永远不会忘记。

I am grateful for my wife, Jeannine Jorgenson, for being extremely patient with the amount of time this required. This book wouldn’t exist without your wise counsel, positivity, and encouragement. I appreciate all the love and care you bring to our family that allows me to work on quests like this. Thank you for always keeping morale high.

感谢我的妻子Jeannine Jorgenson，对我投入大量时间的极大耐心。没有你的明智建议、积极态度和鼓励，这本书不会存在。感谢你为我们的家庭带来的所有爱与关怀，使我能够专注于这样的探索。感谢你始终保持高昂的士气。

I am grateful for author friends who share their expertise and without whom I’d have more confusion and less laughter: Max Olson, Taylor Pearson, James Clear, and Morgan Housel.

感谢那些分享专业知识的作家朋友们，没有你们，我会更加困惑，笑声也会更少：Max Olson、Taylor Pearson、James Clear和Morgan Housel。

I am grateful to my vast crew of beta readers for their time, opinions, and wise advice. Every one of you made valuable contributions to this book, and it wouldn’t be what it is without you. 感谢我庞大的测试读者团队，感谢你们的时间、意见和明智建议。你们每个人都为这本书做出了宝贵贡献，没有你们，这本书不会成为现在的样子。衷心感谢你们每一位：David Senra、George Mack、Ruchir Jajoo、Amanda Orson、Patrick Finley、Alex Wiekowski、Sky King、Megan Darnell、Chase Ilten、Sean Devine、Sean O’Connor、Travis Stoliker、Brady Kurt、Emma Varvaloucas、Raffi Grinberg、Alec Gewirtz、Danielle Krischik、Rame Adi、Everest Brady、Daniel Doyon、Scott Norman、Mitchell Baldridge、Sam Hinkie、Jimmy Donaldson和Nat Eliason。

我依然感激Naval Ravikant和Balaji Srinivasan信任我创作了《Naval Ravikant年鉴》和《Balaji选集》。这两个项目改变了我的人生，也促成了这次机会。

I am grateful for the support of many friends and strangers online who supported and encouraged me throughout this project. My DMs overflow with kind words and eager inquiries. I appreciate every gesture. Your energy helped pull me through the thousand-plus hours it took to create this for you.

感谢许多线上朋友和陌生人的支持与鼓励，伴我完成整个项目。我的私信里充满了善意的话语和热切的询问。感谢你们的每一个举动。你们的能量帮助我度过了创作这本书所需的千余小时。

ABOUT THE AUTHOR关于作者

Eric Jorgenson writes and podcasts about technology, startups, and crafting a bright future. His blog has educated and entertained more than one million readers since 2014. Eric invests in early-stage technology companies. (Please get in touch to invest or pitch a company.)

埃里克·约根森撰写博客和播客，内容涵盖技术、初创企业以及打造光明未来。自2014年以来，他的博客已教育并娱乐了超过一百万读者。埃里克投资于早期科技公司。（如有投资或推介公司意向，请联系他。）

He is also the author of The Almanack of Naval Ravikant, which has been read by millions of people and translated into forty-plus languages to date, and The Anthology of Balaji. He lives in Kansas City with his brilliant wife, Jeannine and son, Archer. Join the author’s email list for more good ideas and special projects at EJorgenson.com.

他还是《纳瓦尔·拉维坎特年鉴》的作者，该书已被数百万人阅读，并被翻译成四十多种语言，以及《巴拉吉文集》的作者。他与才华横溢的妻子珍妮和儿子阿彻一起居住在堪萨斯城。欢迎加入作者的邮件列表，获取更多好点子和特别项目，网址为EJorgenson.com。
